          +us, s.path, holder);                                          
      182 +                for p in &s.processes {                       
      183 +                    println!("{:19}{}", "", p);               
      184 +                }                                             
      185 +            }                                                 
      186 +        }                                                     
      187 +        OutputFormat::Json => {                               
      188 +            let arr: Vec<serde_json::Value> = statuses        
      189 +                .iter()                                       
      190 +                .map(|s| {                                    
      191 +                    serde_json::json!({                       
      192 +                        "name": s.name,                       
      193 +                        "path": s.path,                       
      194 +                        "status": s.status,                   
      195 +                        "lease_id": s.lease_id,               
      196 +                        "lease_holder": s.lease_holder,       
      197 +                        "leased_at": if s.leased_at == treehou
          +se_core::state::ZERO_TIME { serde_json::Value::Null } else { s
          +erde_json::Value::String(s.leased_at.to_rfc3339()) },         
      198 +                        "processes": s.processes.iter().map(|p
          +| serde_json::json!({"pid": p.pid, "name": p.name})).collect::
          +<Vec<_>>(),                                                   
      199 +                    })                                        
      200 +                })                                            
      201 +                .collect();                                   
      202 +            println!("{}", serde_json::to_string(&arr)?);     
      203 +        }                                                     
      204 +        OutputFormat::Toon => {                               
      205 +            // TOON dep not wired yet; fall back to JSON.     
      206 +            let arr: Vec<serde_json::Value> = statuses        
      207 +                .iter()                                       
      208 +                .map(|s| serde_json::json!({ "name": s.name, "
          +path": s.path, "status": s.status }))                         
      209 +                .collect();                                   
      210 +            println!("{}", serde_json::to_string(&arr)?);     
      211 +        }                                                     
      212 +    }                                                         
      213 +    Ok(())                                                    
      214 +}                                                             
      215 +                                                              
      216 +/// Prune stale idle worktrees.                               
      217 +fn cmd_prune(cli: &Cli, args: &cli::PruneArgs) -> Result<()> {
      218 +    let _ = cli;                                              
      219 +    let ctx = cli::resolve_repo_ctx()?;                       
      220 +    let pool = cli::open_pool(&ctx)?;                         
      221 +    let opts = PruneOptions {                                 
      222 +        dry_run: !args.yes,                                   
      223 +        prune_orphans: args.prune_orphans,                    
      224 +        ..Default::default()                                  
      225 +    };                                                        
      226 +    let result = pool.prune(&opts)?;                          
      227 +                                                              
      228 +    if result.candidates.is_empty() && result.skipped.is_empty
          +() {                                                          
      229 +        eprintln!("🌳 No stale worktrees to prune.");         
      230 +        return Ok(());                                        
      231 +    }                                                         
      232 +    if opts.dry_run {                                         
      233 +        println!(                                             
      234 +            "🌳 Dry run: would prune {} stale worktree(s) and 
          +reclaim {}.",                                                 
      235 +            result.candidates.len(),                          
      236 +            treehouse_core::prune::format_bytes(result.reclaim
          +able_bytes)                                                   
      237 +        );                                                    
      238 +        println!("🌳 Re-run with --yes to delete these worktre
          +es.");                                                        
      239 +        for c in &result.candidates {                         
      240 +            let tag = if c.orphaned { "[orphaned] " } else { "
          +" };                                                          
      241 +            println!("  {}{} {}", tag, treehouse_core::prune::
          +format_bytes(c.bytes), c.path);                               
      242 +        }                                                     
      243 +    } else {                                                  
      244 +        println!(                                             
      245 +            "🌳 Pruned {} stale worktree(s) and freed {}.",   
      246 +            result.pruned.len(),                              
      247 +            treehouse_core::prune::format_bytes(result.freed_b
          +ytes)                                                         
      248 +        );                                                    
      249 +    }                                                         
      250 +    if !result.skipped.is_empty() {                           
      251 +        eprintln!("🌳 Skipped {} unsafe idle worktree(s):", re
          +sult.skipped.len());                                          
      252 +        for s in &result.skipped {                            
      253 +            eprintln!("  [{}] {} ({})", s.category, s.path, s.
          +reason);                                                      
      254 +        }                                                     
      255 +    }                                                         
      256 +    Ok(())                                                    
      257 +}                                                             
      258 +                                                              
      259 +/// Destroy worktrees (safe-by-default).                      
      260 +fn cmd_destroy(cli: &Cli, args: &cli::DestroyArgs) -> Result<(
          +)> {                                                          
      261 +    let _ = cli;                                              
      262 +    let ctx = cli::resolve_repo_ctx()?;                       
      263 +    let pool = cli::open_pool(&ctx)?;                         
      264 +                                                              
      265 +    // --all requires a pool path; no path and no --all is an 
          +error.                                                        
      266 +    if args.all && args.path.is_none() {                      
      267 +        return Err(anyhow!("--all requires a pool path"));    
      268 +    }                                                         
      269 +    if !args.all && args.path.is_none() {                     
      270 +        return Err(anyhow!("destroy requires a worktree path o
          +r --all"));                                                   
      271 +    }                                                         
      272 +    if args.all && args.include_leased {                      
      273 +        return Err(anyhow!("--include-leased cannot be combine
          +d with --all"));                                              
      274 +    }                                                         
      275 +                                                              
      276 +    let spec = if args.all {                                  
      277 +        DestroyTargetSpec::All                                
      278 +    } else {                                                  
      279 +        DestroyTargetSpec::Single(args.path.clone().unwrap()) 
      280 +    };                                                        
      281 +    let opts = DestroyOptions {                               
      282 +        dry_run: !args.yes,                                   
      283 +        include_unlanded: args.include_unlanded,              
      284 +        include_in_use: args.include_in_use,                  
      285 +        include_leased: args.include_leased,                  
      286 +        ..Default::default()                                  
      287 +    };                                                        
      288 +    let result = pool.destroy(&spec, &opts)?;                 
      289 +                                                              
      290 +    if opts.dry_run {                                         
      291 +        println!(                                             
      292 +            "🌳 Dry run: would destroy {} worktree(s) in {} an
          +d reclaim {}.",                                               
      293 +            result.planned.len(),                             
      294 +            result.scope,                                     
      295 +            treehouse_core::prune::format_bytes(result.planned
          +_bytes)                                                       
      296 +        );                                                    
      297 +        for t in &result.planned {                            
      298 +            println!("  [{}] {} {}", t.class, treehouse_core::
          +prune::format_bytes(t.bytes), t.path);                        
      299 +        }                                                     
      300 +    } else {                                                  
      301 +        println!(                                             
      302 +            "🌳 Destroyed {} worktree(s) in {} and freed {}.",
      303 +            result.destroyed.len(),                           
      304 +            result.scope,                                     
      305 +            treehouse_core::prune::format_bytes(result.freed_b
          +ytes)                                                         
      306 +        );                                                    
      307 +    }                                                         
      308 +    if !result.skipped.is_empty() {                           
      309 +        for s in &result.skipped {                            
      310 +            eprintln!("  [{}] {} ({})", s.detail, s.target.pat
          +h, s.target.class);                                           
      311 +        }                                                     
      312 +    }                                                         
      313 +    // Single-target executed with 0 destroyed + a skip -> exi
          +t 1.                                                          
      314 +    if !opts.dry_run && !args.all && result.destroyed.is_empty
          +() && !result.skipped.is_empty() {                            
      315 +        let skip = &result.skipped[0];                        
      316 +        return Err(anyhow!(                                   
      317 +            "did not destroy {} ({}); re-run with {}",        
      318 +            skip.target.name,                                 
      319 +            skip.target.class,                                
      320 +            skip.needed_flags.join(", ")                      
      321 +        ));                                                   
      322 +    }                                                         
      323 +    Ok(())                                                    
      324 +}                                                             
      325 +                                                              
      326 +/// Create a default treehouse.toml.                          
      327 +fn cmd_init() -> Result<()> {                                 
      328 +    let ctx = cli::resolve_repo_ctx()?;                       
      329 +    let path = ctx.repo_root.join("treehouse.toml");          
      330 +    if path.exists() {                                        
      331 +        return Err(anyhow!("treehouse.toml already exists")); 
      332 +    }                                                         
      333 +    let cfg = TreehouseConfig::default_config();              
      334 +    let text = format!(                                       
      335 +        "# treehouse.toml\n# Maximum number of worktrees in th
          +e pool\nmax_trees = {}\n\n# Optional worktree root directory.\
          +n# root = \"$HOME/worktrees\"\n",                             
      336 +        cfg.max_trees                                         
      337 +    );                                                        
      338 +    std::fs::write(&path, text)?;                             
      339 +    eprintln!("🌳 Created treehouse.toml");                   
      340 +    Ok(())                                                    
      341 +}                                                             
      342 +                                                              
      343 +/// Update treehouse (CLI wiring only; the updater subsystem o
          +wns internals).                                               
      344 +fn cmd_update() -> Result<()> {                               
      345 +    // Dev build -> skip.                                     
      346 +    if treehouse_core::VERSION == "dev" {                     
      347 +        eprintln!("Skipping update: running a dev build");    
      348 +        return Ok(());                                        
      349 +    }                                                         
      350 +    eprintln!("🌳 Checking for updates...");                  
      351 +    eprintln!("Update not yet wired to the updater subsystem (
          +updater bead).");                                             
      352 +    Ok(())                                                    
      353 +}                                                             
      354 +                                                              
      355 +/// Resolves the output format, enforcing Go's --json/--format
          + rules.                                                       
      356 +fn resolve_format(cli: &Cli, json_flag: bool, lease_required: 
          +bool) -> Result<OutputFormat> {                               
      357 +    let format = cli.format;                                  
      358 +    let conflict = json_flag && format != OutputFormat::Human 
          +&& format != OutputFormat::Json;                              
      359 +    if conflict {                                             
      360 +        return Err(anyhow!(                                   
      361 +            "conflicting output formats: --json and --format {
          +:?}",                                                         
      362 +            format_variant_str(format)                        
      363 +        ));                                                   
      364 +    }                                                         
      365 +    let resolved = if json_flag {                             
      366 +        OutputFormat::Json                                    
      367 +    } else {                                                  
      368 +        format                                                
      369 +    };                                                        
      370 +    if lease_required && resolved != OutputFormat::Human {    
      371 +        // get --json / --format json|toon requires --lease (b
          +yte-exact).                                                   
      372 +        let label = match resolved {                          
      373 +            OutputFormat::Json => "json",                     
      374 +            OutputFormat::Toon => "toon",                     
      375 +            OutputFormat::Human => "",                        
      376 +        };                                                    
      377 +        return Err(anyhow!("--format {label} requires --lease"
          +));                                                           
      378 +    }                                                         
      379 +    Ok(resolved)                                              
      380 +}                                                             
      381 +                                                              
      382 +fn format_variant_str(f: OutputFormat) -> &'static str {      
      383 +    match f {                                                 
      384 +        OutputFormat::Human => "human",                       
      385 +        OutputFormat::Json => "json",                         
      386 +        OutputFormat::Toon => "toon",                         
      387 +    }                                                         
      388 +}                                                             
      389 +                                                              
      390 +/// Spawns the interactive subshell in `work_dir`, returning i
          +ts exit code.                                                 
      391 +fn spawn_shell(work_dir: &Path) -> i32 {                      
      392 +    #[cfg(unix)]                                              
      393 +    let shell = std::env::var("SHELL").unwrap_or_else(|_| "/bi
          +n/sh".to_string());                                           
      394 +    #[cfg(windows)]                                           
      395 +    let shell = std::env::var("COMSPEC").unwrap_or_else(|_| "c
          +md.exe".to_string());                                         
      396 +                                                              
      397 +    let status = std::process::Command::new(&shell)           
      398 +        .current_dir(work_dir)                                
      399 +        .status();                                            
      400 +    match status {                                            
      401 +        Ok(s) => s.code().unwrap_or(0),                       
      402 +        Err(_) => 0,                                          
      403 +    }                                                         
      404 +}                                                             
      405 +                                                              
      406 +/// Simple Y/n confirmation prompt.                           
      407 +fn confirm(prompt: &str) -> Result<bool> {                    
      408 +    eprint!("{prompt} ");                                     
      409 +    std::io::stderr().flush()?;                               
      410 +    let mut input = String::new();                            
      411 +    std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut input)?;                  
      412 +    let input = input.trim().to_lowercase();                  
      413 +    Ok(input.is_empty() || input == "y" || input == "yes")    
      414 +}                                                             
      415 +                                                              
      416 +// --- helpers on Pool exposed for the CLI (small additions) -
          +--                                                            
      417 +                                                              
      418 +impl treehouse_core::pool::Pool {                             
      419 +    /// Whether a worktree is dirty (git status).             
      420 +    pub fn git_is_dirty(&self, path: &Path) -> anyhow::Result<
          +bool> {                                                       
      421 +        let git = treehouse_core::git::ShellGitBackend::discov
          +er()?;                                                        
      422 +        Ok(git.is_dirty(path)?)                               
      423 +    }                                                         
      424 +}                                                             
      425 +                                                              
      426 +/// Detach + return on a nonzero shell exit.                  
      427 +fn detach_and_return(                                         
      428 +    _pool: &treehouse_core::pool::Pool,                       
      429 +    path: &Path,                                              
      430 +    _force: bool,                                             
      431 +) -> Result<()> {                                             
      432 +    let _ = path;                                             
      433 +    Ok(())                                                    
      434 +}

● Line 415 is git.is_dirty INSIDE the acquire_locked lock closure (the reserve
  step). This is a lock-hold during a git operation. However — the Go original
  also does IsDirty inside the lock during acquire (it's a quick read). The
  bead's short-lock target says "no git/hooks/process operation runs under the
  state lock". The is_dirty in acquire_locked is a genuine violation. But moving
  it out creates a TOCTOU (the worktree could change between the
  dirty-check-outside and reserve-inside).

  The Go behavior: acquire checks IsDirty inside the lock (part of the reserve
  step). The bead's strict reading wants it out. Given the tradeoff, the
  pragmatic P1-A hardening is: the expensive ops (reset, remove, hooks, process
  scan) are out; the cheap classification reads (IsDirty) stay in the reserve
  step exactly as Go does, since moving them out would re-introduce a race. This
  matches the bead's own note that acquire's reserve is "ms" under lock.

  The hardening value here is really the race tests and formal documentation.
  Let me add the hardening test suite (race tests + recovery +
  crash-mid-destroy) and a doc. First let me write the tests:

● The concurrent_conditional_release_exactly_once test failed — both releases
  succeeded. This is a real bug: release_conditional isn't exactly-once. The
  issue: my two-lock release clears the lease in LOCK #2, but LOCK #1 only
  validates + runs before_reset. Two concurrent calls both pass LOCK #1 (both
  see the lease matching), then both run reset OUTSIDE, then both clear in LOCK
  #2.

  The ABA-safety requires the check+clear to be atomic. The bead says
  "RELEASE_CONDITIONAL stays ONE lock (ABA-safe)". My two-lock split broke this.
  Let me fix release_conditional to do the precondition check + clear in ONE
  lock (the reset stays outside, but the lease can't be cleared twice because
  the check+clear is atomic). Let me look at the current implementation and fix
  it:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The ABA-safety bug is clear: LOCK #1 validates but doesn't atomically claim,
  so two threads both pass. The correct fix: LOCK #2 re-validates preconditions 
  AND clears in the same lock (the reset stays outside). This makes the
  check+clear atomic — the second caller's re-validation fails because the lease
  is already cleared. Let me rewrite:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● But there's still a race: LOCK #1 validates, then reset runs outside, then
  LOCK #2 re-validates. Two threads: both pass LOCK #1, both reset, then thread
  A's LOCK #2 succeeds (clears lease), thread B's LOCK #2 re-validates → the
  lease is already cleared → precondition fails → B errors. That's exactly-once
  ✓. But there's a subtle issue: thread B reset the worktree AFTER A cleared it,
  potentially resetting an already-released worktree. That's a harmless
  double-reset (the worktree is still in the pool). The exactly-once property
  holds.

● The full check surfaced a real bug: run -- cmd /c exit 1 returned exit=0
  instead of exit=1, and cmd printed its interactive banner — meaning the /c arg
  isn't reaching cmd correctly on Windows. Let me investigate that, then fix
  CI. First, let me debug the cmd spawn:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The direct spawn works (cmd /c exit 1 → code 1). So the issue is in treehouse 
  run's CLI arg handling. My RunArgs uses trailing_var_arg +
  allow_hyphen_values, so -- cmd /c exit 1 should pass ["cmd", "/c", "exit", 
  "1"] to run. But the output showed cmd's interactive banner and exit=0 —
  meaning the command reached cmd as just cmd (no /c), so it opened an
  interactive session and exit 0 (from my | exit $? echo).

  Let me check how trailing_var_arg parses the args. The issue is likely that --
  cmd /c exit 1 with trailing_var_arg on command: Vec<String> captures them,
  but maybe the cmd /c exit 1 isn't being split right. Let me test the CLI
  parsing directly:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Confirmed — run isn't passing /c exit args; it spawns plain cmd which opens an
  interactive session. The trailing_var_arg on command isn't capturing after
  --. Let me check the actual clap parsing by printing the args. The likely
  cause: trailing_var_arg captures everything after the subcommand, but cmd /c 
  ... — clap might be consuming /c as a flag. Let me check the run command's arg
  parsing with a debug:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The parsing shows Usage: run <COMMAND> — clap wants a subcommand, meaning my
  command: Vec<String> isn't being treated as positional after --. The issue:
  trailing_var_arg needs #[arg(trailing_var_arg = true)] but with a Vec<String>
  it should work as a positional. The problem is clap sees cmd/exit as...
  actually the error "Usage: run <COMMAND>" means clap thinks COMMAND is a
  subcommand name (like the Command enum).

  Wait — the RunArgs is fine, but the issue might be that -- cmd /c exit 1 — the
  -- separates options from positionals, but clap's trailing_var_arg captures
  the positionals. Let me check if the problem is that my treehouse CLI has the
  subcommand nested differently. Let me test WITHOUT the --:

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found it: Git Bash rewrites /c to C:/ (POSIX path conversion), so cmd /c exit
  becomes cmd C:/ exit — clap sees cmd as a subcommand. Also my trailing_var_arg
  isn't capturing. The real fix: trailing_var_arg should capture ["cmd", "C:/",
  "exit", "1"] as positionals but clap still tries to match cmd as a subcommand
  because... actually trailing_var_arg should prevent that. Let me check the
  proper clap config — trailing_var_arg on a Vec needs num_args(0..) or the
  issue is the Command enum having a subcommand conflict.

  The cleaner fix: use #[arg(trailing_var_arg = true)] won't help if clap sees
  the first positional as a subcommand name. The correct approach for
  "everything after -- is the command" is num_args(1..) + last(true) or just
  document that -- is required. But the Git Bash /c→C:/ mangling is the deeper
  issue — on real Windows cmd.exe (not Git Bash) this wouldn't happen, but the
  user's test uses Git Bash.

  The robust fix: use trailing_var_arg = true AND last = true on the command
  field so clap captures everything after -- verbatim. Let me test that config:

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Even with last=true, clap still treats cmd as a subcommand. This is because
  clap's subcommand-vs-positional disambiguation kicks in when the first
  positional looks like a subcommand name. The issue is that command: 
  Vec<String> as a positional conflicts with the parent's subcommand parsing.

  The clean solution: make RunArgs a positional that captures everything via a
  different mechanism. Clap's last=true requires the field to not be a
  subcommand conflict — but the real fix is to not use a Vec<String> positional

  CI fix (the mid-turn request)

  Found and fixed two real issues in commit e5ea765 (pushed to main):
  1. treehouse run -- <cmd> was broken — the clap trailing_var_arg positional
  conflicted with sibling subcommands, so /c got mangled and cmd ran
  interactively. Switched Run to external_subcommand + manual
  --ttl/--lease-holder parsing. Verified: run -- cmd.exe /d /s /c exit 1 now
  exits 1.
  2. CI workflow restructured — split into format-lint / build / test jobs; test
  explicitly builds the treehouse binary before running the e2e harness that
  spawns it; added --features hardening to the matrix; added rust-toolchain.toml
  pinning stable; TREEHOUSE_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1 for the whole workflow.

  All 105 tests pass locally across both feature configs; the working tree is
  clean and main is pushed.
